<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-74930A6F-A20D-49EC-9CE0-3CBD9F1EAF84'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The price of a one-day ticket to a theme park is $\pounds 37.60$. Joe buys a ticket and experiences $8$ 
rides during the day. Work out the average cost per ride.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average cost per ride}    &= \dfrac {37.6} {8} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {37.6 \times 10} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {376} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {8 \times 47} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 47} {\cancel 8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {47} {10} \\\\
                                &= \pounds 4.7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 4.7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average cost per ride}    &= \dfrac {37.6} {8} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {37.6 \times 10} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {376} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {8 \times 47} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 47} {\cancel 8 \times 10} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {47} {10} \\\\
                                &= \pounds 4.7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 4.7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Rishi pays an additional $\pounds 31$ for a special pass which gives front of line access to all rides throughout the day. 
He experiences $14$ rides. Does the special pass provide value for money? Give reasons for your answer.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using the values from the answer above,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 14 tickets}       &= 14 \times 4.7 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 65.8 \\\\
\text{Money saved}              &= 65.8 - 31 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 34.8
\end{aligned}
$

Yes, the special pass provides value for money as Rishi saved $\pounds 34.8$ in it

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 34.8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using the values from the answer above,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 14 tickets}       &= 14 \times 4.7 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 65.8 \\\\
\text{Money saved}              &= 65.8 - 31 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 34.8
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Yes, the special pass provides value for money as Rishi saved $\pounds 34.8$ in it
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 34.8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Active Eight had $4$ runners in their team.

Find the mean time for each runner in this team by dividing their total time by $4$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean time}    &= \dfrac {51.2} {4} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {512} {4 \times 10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {4 \times 128} {4 \times 10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 128} {\cancel 4 \times 10} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {128} {10} \\\\
                    &= 12.8 \ \text{s}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12.8 \ \text{s}$

$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean number of people}    &= \dfrac {60} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {12 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>

                            &= \dfrac {11} {2} \\\\
                            &= 5.5
\end {aligned}
$


Let's look for the value that occurs the most.

| Number    | Count     |
|:------:   |:-----:    |
|   $2$     |    $1$    |
|   $5$     |    $2$    |
|   $6$     |    $1$    |

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Two numbers have a mean of $12$ and a range of $6$. 

What are the two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


ABBR: $\text{Range} = \text{Highest number} - \text{Lowest number}$


Let the first number $= a$

Let the second number $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {a + b} {2}      &= 12 \\\\
a + b                   &= 12 \times 2 \\\\
a + b                   &= 24 & (1) \\\\
b - a                   &= 6  & (2)
\end {aligned}
$

Let's use elimination to reduce it to just one variable. Add the two equations to eliminate $a$.

$
\begin {aligned}
a + b                   &= 24 \\\\
b - a                   &= 6  \\\\
a + b + b -a            &= 24 + 6 \\\\
2b                      &= 30 \\\\
b                       &= \dfrac {30} {2} \\\\
b                       &= 15
\end {aligned}
$

Let's substitute the value of $b$ in one of the initial equations to find the value of $a$.

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b                       &= 24  \\\\
a + 15                      &= 24 \\\\
a                           &= 24 - 15 \\\\
a                           &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>
Let the second number $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,

\begin {aligned}

\end {aligned}
$

\end {aligned}
$

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

a + 15                      &= 24 \\\\
a                           &= 24 - 15 \\\\
a                           &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

Write down a possible set of five numbers.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Numbers}  &= 5, 5, 6, 9, 10 \\\\
\text {Mean}    &= \dfrac {5 + 5 + 6 + 9 + 10} {5} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {35} {5} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {5 \times 7} {5} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 7} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
                &= 7 \\\\
\text {Median}  &= 6 \\\\
\text {Mode}    &= 5
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5, 5, 6, 9, 10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

                &= 7 \\\\

<div class='question question'>

Five pupils enter a maths competition.

Their scores in the Mental Challenge are:

$18 \quad 20 \quad 15 \quad 12 \quad 20$

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


For these $5$ scores, work out:

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

the mode

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Lets find out the score that occurs the most.

| Number    | Count     |
|:------:   |:-----:    |
|   $12$    |    $1$    |
|   $15$    |    $1$    |
|   $18$    |    $1$    |
|   $20$    |    $2$    |

Mode $= 20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Lets find out the score that occurs the most.
<emptyline>
| Number    | Count     |
|:------:   |:-----:    |
|   $12$    |    $1$    |
|   $15$    |    $1$    |
|   $18$    |    $1$    |
|   $20$    |    $2$    |
<emptyline>
Mode $= 20$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

the median

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


Let's arrange the marks scored in ascending order.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Marks scored}           &= 18, \quad 20, \quad 15, \quad 12, \quad 20 \\\\
\text{Ordered marks scored}   &= 12, \quad 15, \quad \maroonC
{18}, \quad 20, \quad 20 \\\\
\text{Median}                 &= \dfrac {5 + 10} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {15} {2} \\\\
                              &= 7.5
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_median
<emptyline>
Let's arrange the marks scored in ascending order.
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Marks scored}           &= 18, \quad 20, \quad 15, \quad 12, \quad 20 \\\\
\text{Ordered marks scored}   &= 12, \quad 15, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{18}, \quad 20, \quad 20 \\\\
\text{Median}                 &= \dfrac {5 + 10} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {15} {2} \\\\
                              &= 7.5
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$18$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

the mean

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean}             &= \dfrac {12 + 15 + 18 + 20 + 20} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {85} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {17 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {17 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
                        &= 17
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$17$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean}             &= \dfrac {12 + 15 + 18 + 20 + 20} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {85} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {17 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {17 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
                        &= 17
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$17$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question subsubquestion'>

the median temperature

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


Let's arrange the temperatures in ascending order.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Temperatures}           &= 3, \quad 9, \quad 21, \quad 29, \quad 24, \quad 11 \\\\
\text{Ordered temperatures}   &= 3, \quad 9, \quad \maroonC
{11}, \quad \maroonC
{21}, \quad 24, \quad 29 \\\\
\text{Median temperature}     &= \dfrac {11 + 21} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {32} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {2 \times 16} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 16} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                              &= 16 \degree \text{C}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16 \degree \text{C}$

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The cost of a bottle of lemonade in four different shops is

$42 \text{p}, 38 \text{p}, 40 \text{p}$ and $36 \text{p}$.

Calculate the mean price of a bottle of lemonade.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean price}       &= \dfrac {40 + 42 + 38 + 36} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {156} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {39 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {39 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
                        &= 39 \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$39 \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean price}       &= \dfrac {40 + 42 + 38 + 36} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {156} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {39 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {39 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
                        &= 39 \text{p}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$39 \text{p}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

Freshly-squeezed lemonade costs $57 \text{p}$ a bottle.

How much would $13$ bottles of this lemonade cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of lemonade}     &= 57 \text{p} \times 13 \\\\
                            &= \pounds \dfrac {57} {100} \times 13 \\\\
                            &= 0.57 \times 13 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 7.14
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 7.14$

                            &= \pounds 7.14
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-2E4B6B46-9C74-48E1-9CCC-D09491A7E79D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Calculate the mean of these numbers.

$9 \quad 14 \quad 7 \quad 17 \quad 8$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Mean} &= \dfrac {9 + 14 + 7 + 17 + 8} {5} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {55} {5} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {11 \times 5} {5} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {11 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
             &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Mean} &= \dfrac {9 + 14 + 7 + 17 + 8} {5} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {55} {5} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {11 \times 5} {5} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {11 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
             &= 11
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-24E2739E-F667-4506-94E5-CE248BC1F021'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Mrs Fowler keeps chickens.

The table shows how many eggs her chickens laid on each weekday last week.

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


The mean number of eggs laid from Monday to Friday last week was $6$.

How many eggs were laid on Tuesday?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the number of eggs laid on Tuesday $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {4 + a + 4 + 3 + 11} {5}         &= 6 \\\\
\dfrac {a + 22} {5}                     &= 6 \\\\
a + 22                                  &= 6 \times 5 \\\\
a + 22                                  &= 30 \\\\
a                                       &= 30 - 22 \\\\
a                                       &= 8
\end {aligned}
$

$8$ eggs were laid on Tuesday.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let the number of eggs laid on Tuesday $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {4 + a + 4 + 3 + 11} {5}         &= 6 \\\\
\dfrac {a + 22} {5}                     &= 6 \\\\
a + 22                                  &= 6 \times 5 \\\\
a + 22                                  &= 30 \\\\
a                                       &= 30 - 22 \\\\
a                                       &= 8
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$8$ eggs were laid on Tuesday.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7C2067DD-CE34-49B3-81E9-34FB9F3C9414'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Tom downloads $3$ music tracks.
The length of each track is shown in the table below.

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


track     length

track 1  $4 \ \text{min} 45 \ \text{sec}$

track 2  $5 \ \text{min} 5  \ \text{sec}$

track 3  $4 \ \text{min} 40 \ \text{sec}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the total length of the $3$ tracks?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total length}    &= (4 \times 60 + 45) + (5 \times 60 + 5) + (4 \times 60 + 40) \\\\
                        &= 240 + 45 + 300 + 5 + 240 + 40 \\\\
                        &= 870 \ \text{s} \\\\
                        &= 840 \ \text{s} + 30 \ \text{s} \\\\
                        &= 60 \times 14 \ \text{s} + 30 \ \text{s} \\\\
                        &= 14 \ \text{min} + 30 \ \text{s} \\\\
                        &= 14 \ \text{min} \ 30 \ \text{s}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14 \ \text{min} \ 30 \ \text{s}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total length}    &= (4 \times 60 + 45) + (5 \times 60 + 5) + (4 \times 60 + 40) \\\\
                        &= 240 + 45 + 300 + 5 + 240 + 40 \\\\
                        &= 870 \ \text{s} \\\\
                        &= 840 \ \text{s} + 30 \ \text{s} \\\\
                        &= 60 \times 14 \ \text{s} + 30 \ \text{s} \\\\
                        &= 14 \ \text{min} + 30 \ \text{s} \\\\
                        &= 14 \ \text{min} \ 30 \ \text{s}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$14 \ \text{min} \ 30 \ \text{s}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the mean (average) length of the track?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Using value from the answer above,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Mean length} &= \dfrac {870} {3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 290} {3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 290} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                    &= 290 \ \text{s} \\\\
                    &= 240 \ \text{s} + 50 \ \text{s} \\\\
                    &= 4 \times 60 \ \text{s} + 50 \ \text{s} \\\\
                    &= 4 \ \text{min} + 50 \ \text{s} \\\\
                    &= 4 \ \text{min} \ 50 \ \text{s}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4 \ \text{min} \ 50 \ \text{s}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Using value from the answer above,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Mean length} &= \dfrac {870} {3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 290} {3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 290} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                    &= 290 \ \text{s} \\\\
                    &= 240 \ \text{s} + 50 \ \text{s} \\\\
                    &= 4 \times 60 \ \text{s} + 50 \ \text{s} \\\\
                    &= 4 \ \text{min} + 50 \ \text{s} \\\\
                    &= 4 \ \text{min} \ 50 \ \text{s}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4 \ \text{min} \ 50 \ \text{s}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out her total practice time for these $5$ days.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total practice time}     &= \dfrac {10 + 14 + 6 + 10 + 15} {5} \\\\
                                &= 55 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$55 \ \text{min}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total practice time}     &= \dfrac {10 + 14 + 6 + 10 + 15} {5} \\\\
                                &= 55 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out her mean (average) practice time for these $5$ days.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Using value from the answer above,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Average practice time}   &= \dfrac {55} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {5 \times 11} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 11} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                &= 11 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11 \ \text{min}$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Vanessa also practised on Saturday.

Her mean practice time for all $6$ days was $15 \ \text{minutes}$.

Work out for how many minutes Vanessa practised on Saturday.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let number of minutes Vanessa practiced on Saturday $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {10 + 14 + 6 + 10 + 15 + a} {6}          &= 15 \\\\
\dfrac {55 + a} {6}                             &= 15 \\\\
55 + a                                          &= 15 \times 6 \\\\
55 + a                                          &= 90 \\\\
a                                               &= 90 - 55 \\\\
a                                               &= 35 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$

Vanessa practised on Saturday for $35 \ \text{min}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$35 \ \text{min}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}

\dfrac {55 + a} {6}                             &= 15 \\\\
55 + a                                          &= 15 \times 6 \\\\
55 + a                                          &= 90 \\\\
a                                               &= 90 - 55 \\\\
a                                               &= 35 \ \text{min}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Vanessa practised on Saturday for $35 \ \text{min}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$35 \ \text{min}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

*Chocolate Heaven* sells $10$ different types of hand-made chocolates.

The price of each type of dark chocolate is shown in the table below.

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the total cost of the $5$ dark chocolates?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Total cost}               &= 28 + 35 + 27 + 25 + 30 \\\\
                                &= 145 \text{p} \\\\
                                &= \pounds 1.45
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.45$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>

\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>

</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 1.45$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the mean (average) cost of a dark chocolate?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Using value from the answer above,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Mean cost}   &= \dfrac {145} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {5 \times 29} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 29} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                    &= 29 \text{p}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$29 \text{p}$

</div>
</div>

<emptyline>

                    &= \dfrac {5 \times 29} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 29} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                    &= 29 \text{p}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

For each type of chocolate, there is a milk chocolate version which costs 2 \text{p}$ less than the dark chocolate version.

Write down the mean cost of a milk chocolate.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Mean cost}   &= \dfrac {28 - 2 + 35 - 2 + 27 - 2 + 25 - 2 + 30 - 2} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {26 + 33 + 25 + 23 + 28} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {135} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 27} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                    &= 27 \text{p}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$27 \text{p}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Mean cost}   &= \dfrac {28 - 2 + 35 - 2 + 27 - 2 + 25 - 2 + 30 - 2} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {26 + 33 + 25 + 23 + 28} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {135} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 27} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                    &= 27 \text{p}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

Using your answer to part (a) or otherwise, work out the cost of all $10$ chocolates.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


Using value from the answer above,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total cost}      &= \text{Dark chocolate} + \text{Milk chocolate} \\\\
                        &= 145 + 135 \\\\
                        &= 280 \text{p} \\\\
                        &= \pounds 2.8
\end {aligned}
$

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the total time that Yoshi takes to make the $4$ origami models.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total time taken}        &= (5 \times 60 + 20) + (4 \times 60 + 44) + (2 \times 60 + 3) + (8 \times 60 + 17) \\\\
                                &= (300 + 20) + (240 + 44) + (120 + 3) + (480 + 17) \\\\
                                &= 320 + 284 + 123 + 497 \\\\
                                &= 1224 \ \text{s} \\\\
                                &= 1200 \ \text{s} + 24 \ \text{s} \\\\
                                &= 20 \times 60 \ \text{s} + 24 \ \text{s} \\\\
                                &= 20 \ \text{min} \ 24 \ \text{s}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20 \ \text{min} \ 24 \ \text{s}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total time taken}        &= (5 \times 60 + 20) + (4 \times 60 + 44) + (2 \times 60 + 3) + (8 \times 60 + 17) \\\\
                                &= (300 + 20) + (240 + 44) + (120 + 3) + (480 + 17) \\\\
                                &= 320 + 284 + 123 + 497 \\\\
                                &= 1224 \ \text{s} \\\\
                                &= 1200 \ \text{s} + 24 \ \text{s} \\\\
                                &= 20 \times 60 \ \text{s} + 24 \ \text{s} \\\\
                                &= 20 \ \text{min} \ 24 \ \text{s}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20 \ \text{min} \ 24 \ \text{s}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the mean (average) time taken to create one origami model?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Using value from the answer above

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Average time taken}  &= \dfrac {1224} {4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {4 \times 306} {4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 306} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                            &= 306 \ \text{s} \\\\
                            &= 300 + 6 \ \text{s} \\\\
                            &= 5 \times 60 \ \text{s} + 6 \ \text{s} \\\\
                            &= 5 \ \text{min} \ 6 \ \text{s}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5 \ \text{min} \ 6 \ \text{s}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 306} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                            &= 306 \ \text{s} \\\\
                            &= 300 + 6 \ \text{s} \\\\
                            &= 5 \times 60 \ \text{s} + 6 \ \text{s} \\\\

$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the range of times that Yoshi takes to make an origami model?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Range} = \text{Highest number} - \text{Lowest number}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Range}   &= 8 \times 60 + 17 - (2 \times 60 + 3) \\\\
                &= 480 + 17 - (120 + 3) \\\\
                &= 497 - 123 \\\\
                &= 374 \\\\
                &= 360 + 14 \\\\
                &= 6 \times 60 + 14 \\\\
                &= 6 \ \text{min} + 14 \ \text{s} \\\\
                &= 6 \ \text{min} \ 14 \ \text{s}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6 \ \text{min} \ 14 \ \text{s}$

                &= 374 \\\\
                &= 360 + 14 \\\\
                &= 6 \times 60 + 14 \\\\
                &= 6 \ \text{min} + 14 \ \text{s} \\\\
                &= 6 \ \text{min} \ 14 \ \text{s}
\end {aligned}
$

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Six members of the Clark family have the following shoe sizes:

$9 \quad 1 \dfrac{1}{2} \quad 5 \quad 1 \dfrac{1}{2} \quad 3 \quad 4$

For the Clark family, work out:

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<ul class='subsubquestion lower-roman'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

the median shoe size

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


Let's arrange the shoe sizes in ascending order.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Shoe sizes}             &= 9, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 5, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 3, \quad 4 \\\\
\text{Ordered shoe sizes}     &= 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad \maroonC
{3}, \quad \maroonC
{4}, \quad 5, \quad 9 \\\\
\text{Median shoe size}       &= \dfrac {3 + 4} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7} {2} \\\\
                              &= 3.5
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_median
<emptyline>
Let's arrange the shoe sizes in ascending order.
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Shoe sizes}             &= 9, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 5, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 3, \quad 4 \\\\
\text{Ordered shoe sizes}     &= 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad 1 \dfrac {1} {2}, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{3}, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{4}, \quad 5, \quad 9 \\\\
\text{Median shoe size}       &= \dfrac {3 + 4} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7} {2} \\\\
                              &= 3.5
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3.5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

the mean shoe size

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean shoe size}   &= \dfrac {1 \dfrac {1} {2} + 1 \dfrac {1} {2} + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {6 \times 4} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 4} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                        &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean shoe size}   &= \dfrac {1 \dfrac {1} {2} + 1 \dfrac {1} {2} + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 + 3 + 4 + 5 + 9} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {6 \times 4} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 4} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                        &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>
</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Jane has recorded the shoe size of every girl in her class.

Her results are shown in the table below.

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


Jane says “The mode is $12$”.

Annabel says “The mode is $4$”.

Who is correct? Give a reason for your answer.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Annabel is correct because $12$ is the value of maximum frequency and $4$ is the modal size.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Annabel

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Annabel is correct because $12$ is the value of maximum frequency and $4$ is the modal size.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Annabel
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-46AD22C1-138E-4E6D-892A-7848B1C99046'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Joanne’s marks in five spelling tests are: 

$7 \quad 9 \quad 8 \quad 7 \quad 6$

What is the range of her marks?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Range} = \text{Highest number} - \text{Lowest number}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Range}   &= 9 - 6 \\\\
                &= 3
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_range
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Range}   &= 9 - 6 \\\\
                &= 3
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-ECB9BF93-8E27-42A0-873B-87BFA9D37BB8'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Janet’s marks on five mental arithmetic tests are:

$15 \quad 19 \quad 13 \quad 18 \quad 20$

What is her mean (average) mark?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average marks}            &= \dfrac {15 + 19 + 13 + 18 + 20} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {85} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {17 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                &= 17
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$17$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average marks}            &= \dfrac {15 + 19 + 13 + 18 + 20} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {85} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {17 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                &= 17
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$17$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B031DDB3-A80E-4854-9094-75EEF82ADCDA'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

In a list of numbers:

the **mode** is the number which occurs the most often;

the **median** is the number which is in the middle of the list when the numbers have been put in order;

the **mean** is the sum of all the numbers divided by how many numbers there are in the list.

For example, for the list $1, 4, 7, 1, 2$

mode = $1$, median = $2$, mean = $\dfrac{15}{5} = 3$.

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the mode, the median, and the mean for the list $8, 5, 2, 16, 6, 5, 7$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


As per the question,

| Number    | Count     |
|:------:   |:-----:    |
|   $2$     |    $1$    |
|   $5$     |    $2$    |
|   $6$     |    $1$    |
|   $7$     |    $1$    |
|   $8$     |    $1$    |
|   $16$    |    $1$    |

Mode $= 5$

Let's arrange the numbers in ascending order.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Numbers}           &= 8, \quad 5, \quad 2, \quad 16, \quad 6, \quad 5, \quad 7 \\\\
\text{Ordered numbers}   &= 2, \quad 5, \quad 5, \quad \maroonC
{6}, \quad 7, \quad 8, \quad 16 \\\\
\text{Median}            &= 6
\end {aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Mean}    &= \dfrac {2 + 5 + 5 + 6 + 7 + 8 + 16} {7} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {49} {7} \\\\
                &= 7
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Mode $=$ $5$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Median $=$ $6$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Mean $=$ $7$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down a list of three whole numbers for which the mode is $4$ and the mean is $6$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the three whole numbers $= a, b, c$

As per the question,

Mode $= 4$

At least two numbers will be $= 4$

Let a = b = 4

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a + b + c} {3}                  &= 6 \\\\
\dfrac {4 + 4 + c} {3}                  &= 6 \\\\
\dfrac {8 + c} {3}                      &= 6 \\\\
8 + c                                   &= 6 \times 3 \\\\
8 + c                                   &= 18 \\\\
c                                       &= 18 - 8 \\\\
c                                       &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

Three whole numbers $= 4, 4, 10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4, 4, 10$

</div>
</div>

8 + c                                   &= 18 \\\\

c                                       &= 10
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


The five whole numbers are $= 1, 2, 4, 9, 9$

|   $2$     |    $1$    |
|   $4$     |    $1$    |
|   $9$     |    $2$    |

$
\begin {aligned}
text{Marks scored}            &= 1, \quad 2, \quad 4, \quad 9, \quad 9 \\\\
\text{Ordered numbers}        &= 1, \quad 2, \quad \maroonC
{4}, \quad 9, \quad 9 \\\\
\text{Median}                 &= 9
\end {aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean}     &= \dfrac {1 + 2 + 4 + 9 + 9} {5} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {25} {5} \\\\
                &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


List $1: 1, \quad 1, \quad 1, \quad 1, \quad 6$

List $2: 1, \quad 1, \quad 1, \quad 3, \quad 4$

List $3: 1, \quad 1, \quad 1, \quad 2, \quad 5$

Let's verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean for list 1}          &= \dfrac {1 + 1 + 1 + 1 + 6} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {10} {5} \\\\
                                &= 2 \\\\
\text{Mean for list 2}          &= \dfrac {1 + 1 + 1 + 3 + 4} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {10} {5} \\\\
                                &= 2 \\\\
\text{Mean for list 3}          &= \dfrac {1 + 1 + 1 + 2 + 5} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {10} {5} \\\\
                                &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

|   $1$     |    $4$    |
|   $2$     |    $1$    |
|   $5$     |    $1$    |

Mode $= 1$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-15AAA21D-323A-42DC-BF5A-82371B49D4EB'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Four boys have an average mass of $50 \ \text{kg}$ and 
six girls have an average mass of $40 \ \text{kg}$. What 
is the average mass of the ten children?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average mass}     &= \dfrac {\text{Mass of boys} + \text{Mass of girls}} {\text{Total children}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {4 \times 50 + 6 \times 40} {4 + 6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {200 + 240} {10} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {440} {10} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {44 \cancel{0}} {1 \cancel{0}} \\\\
                        &= 44  \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$44 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average mass}     &= \dfrac {\text{Mass of boys} + \text{Mass of girls}} {\text{Total children}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {4 \times 50 + 6 \times 40} {4 + 6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {200 + 240} {10} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {440} {10} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {44 \cancel{0}} {1 \cancel{0}} \\\\
                        &= 44  \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$44 \ \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-2C9FD9EC-F28E-4BA1-8D8C-70118055B9D2'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

For the first $12$ months after I bought my new phone I downloaded $4$ apps to it each month. Over the next $7$ months I only downloaded $1$ app each month, but this month I downloaded $5$ new apps. What is the average number of apps I have downloaded each month while I have had the phone?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average number of apps}       &= \dfrac { (12 \times 4) + (7 \times 1) + (1 \times 5) } { 12 + 7 + 1 } \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac { 48 + 7 + 5 } { 20 } \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {60} {20} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {20 \times 3} {20} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel{20} \times 3} {\cancel{20}} \\\\
                                    &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average number of apps}       &= \dfrac { (12 \times 4) + (7 \times 1) + (1 \times 5) } { 12 + 7 + 1 } \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac { 48 + 7 + 5 } { 20 } \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {60} {20} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {20 \times 3} {20} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel{20} \times 3} {\cancel{20}} \\\\
                                    &= 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B1663F8E-5D97-46AD-80FC-DFEBC4114EED'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

On a wet week in Rusholme the mean (or average) rainfall over the five days from Monday to Friday was $12 \ \text{mm}$. 
On Monday, when the rain was heaviest there was $22 \ \text{mm}$, but on Friday there was only $5 \ \text{mm}$ of rain.  

What was the mean rainfall on the other three days?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the average rainfall for other three days $= r$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {22 + 3r + 5} {5}                     &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac {27 + 3r} {5}                         &= 12 \\\\
27 + 3r                                      &= 12 \times 5 \\\\
27 + 3r                                      &= 60 \\\\
3r                                           &= 60 - 27 \\\\
3r                                           &= 33 \\\\
r                                            &= \dfrac {33} {3} \\\\
r                                            &= \dfrac {11 \times 3} {3} \\\\
r                                            &= \dfrac {11 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
r                                            &= 11 \ \text{mm}
\end{aligned}
$

Average rainfall $= 11 \ \text{mm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11\ \text {mm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let the average rainfall for other three days $= r$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {22 + 3r + 5} {5}                     &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac {27 + 3r} {5}                         &= 12 \\\\
27 + 3r                                      &= 12 \times 5 \\\\
27 + 3r                                      &= 60 \\\\
3r                                           &= 60 - 27 \\\\
3r                                           &= 33 \\\\
r                                            &= \dfrac {33} {3} \\\\
r                                            &= \dfrac {11 \times 3} {3} \\\\
r                                            &= \dfrac {11 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3}} \\\\
r                                            &= 11 \ \text{mm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Average rainfall $= 11 \ \text{mm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$11\ \text {mm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-00C35702-5C9A-410E-8258-FD4D136875E0'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The mean length of a crocodile is $7.5 \ \text{m}$ and the mean length 
of an alligator is $4 \ \text{m}$. If I have $4$ crocodiles and $6$ alligators 
what would be the mean length of all $10$ creatures together?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean length}      &= \dfrac {4 \times 7.5 + 6 \times 4} {4 + 6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {30 + 24} {10} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {54} {10} \\\\
                        &= 5.4  \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5.4 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean length}      &= \dfrac {4 \times 7.5 + 6 \times 4} {4 + 6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {30 + 24} {10} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {54} {10} \\\\
                        &= 5.4  \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5.4 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The **average** height of three boys is $1.29 \ \text{metres}$.  A fourth boy Stephen, whose height is $1.21 \ \text{metres}$, joins the group. Find the new **average** height of the group.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total height of 3 boys}   &= 3 \times 1.29 \\\\
                                &= 3.87 \ \text {m} \\\\
\text{New average height}       &= \dfrac {3.87 + 1.21} {4} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {5.08} {4} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {508} {4 \times 100} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {4 \times 127} {4 \times 100} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 127} {\cancel 4 \times 100} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {127} {100} \\\\
                                &= 1.27 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.27 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total height of 3 boys}   &= 3 \times 1.29 \\\\
                                &= 3.87 \ \text {m} \\\\
\text{New average height}       &= \dfrac {3.87 + 1.21} {4} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {5.08} {4} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {508} {4 \times 100} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {4 \times 127} {4 \times 100} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 127} {\cancel 4 \times 100} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {127} {100} \\\\
                                &= 1.27 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>

</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Two more boys, Richard and Nigel, join the group.  The new **average** height of all six boys in the group is $1.28 \ \text{metres}$. If Richard is $1.26 \ \text {metres}$, how tall is Nigel?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the height of Nigel $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1.29 \times 3 + 1.21 + 1.26 + a} {6}    &= 1.28 \\\\
\dfrac {3.87 + 1.21 + 1.26 + a} {6}             &= 1.28 \\\\
\dfrac {6.34 + a} {6}                           &= 1.28 \\\\
6.34 + a                                        &= 1.28 \times 6 \\\\
6.34 + a                                        &= 7.68 \\\\
a                                               &= 7.68 - 6.34 \\\\
a                                               &= 1.34 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

The height of Nigel $= 1.34 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.34 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let the height of Nigel $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1.29 \times 3 + 1.21 + 1.26 + a} {6}    &= 1.28 \\\\
\dfrac {3.87 + 1.21 + 1.26 + a} {6}             &= 1.28 \\\\
\dfrac {6.34 + a} {6}                           &= 1.28 \\\\
6.34 + a                                        &= 1.28 \times 6 \\\\
6.34 + a                                        &= 7.68 \\\\
a                                               &= 7.68 - 6.34 \\\\
a                                               &= 1.34 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The height of Nigel $= 1.34 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1.34 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>

If there are $31$ pupils in Mr Smith’s class, how many do not own any of the above animals?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of pupils with no pets}    &= 31 - 4 - 8 - 6 - 3 \\\\
                                        &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

\text{Number of pupils with no pets}    &= 31 - 4 - 8 - 6 - 3 \\\\
                                        &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of the children with pets, own a dog?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of dog owners}           &= \dfrac {6} {4 + 8 + 6 + 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {6} {21} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 3} {7 \times 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 3} {7 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2} {7}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {2} {7}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of dog owners}           &= \dfrac {6} {4 + 8 + 6 + 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {6} {21} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times 3} {7 \times 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \times \cancel 3} {7 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2} {7}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {2} {7}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-2C8704E9-28A3-4E26-8769-C455471A9B0F'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

When each diagram below is complete the number in the middle of each group of $3$ adjoining cells is the average of its two neighbours. Fill in the missing numbers.


| $5$     |       | $9$     |       |       |
|:---:    |:---:    |:---:    |:---:    |:---:    |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the missing numbers $= a, b, c$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a                       &= \dfrac {5 + 9} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {14} {2} \\\\
a                       &= 7 \\\\
\dfrac {a + b} {2}      &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac {7 + b} {2}      &= 9 \\\\
7 + b                   &= 9 \times 2 \\\\
7 + b                   &= 18 \\\\
b                       &= 18 - 7 \\\\
b                       &= 11 \\\\
\dfrac {9 + c} {2}      &= b \\\\
\dfrac {9 + c} {2}      &= 11 \\\\
9 + c                   &= 11 \times 2 \\\\
9 + c                   &= 22 \\\\
c                       &= 22 - 9 \\\\
c                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

The missing numbers $= 7, 11, 13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7, 11, 13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let the missing numbers $= a, b, c$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a                       &= \dfrac {5 + 9} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {14} {2} \\\\
a                       &= 7 \\\\
\dfrac {a + b} {2}      &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac {7 + b} {2}      &= 9 \\\\
7 + b                   &= 9 \times 2 \\\\
7 + b                   &= 18 \\\\
b                       &= 18 - 7 \\\\
b                       &= 11 \\\\
\dfrac {9 + c} {2}      &= b \\\\
\dfrac {9 + c} {2}      &= 11 \\\\
9 + c                   &= 11 \times 2 \\\\
9 + c                   &= 22 \\\\
c                       &= 22 - 9 \\\\
c                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The missing numbers $= 7, 11, 13$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7, 11, 13$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-3B8ED099-3816-4CA4-A1A7-3046A7BEB6E6'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The test scores of five pupils were:

$9,\:\:\:10,\:\:\:9,\:\:\:7,\:\:\:5$

Whats is the mean (or average) score?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average score}    &= \dfrac {9 + 10 + 9 + 7 + 5} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {40} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                        &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average score}    &= \dfrac {9 + 10 + 9 + 7 + 5} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {40} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                        &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

When a sixth pupil sat the test the mean score stayed exactly the same.  

Find the score of the sixth pupil. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the score of sixth pupil $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {9 + 10 + 9 + 7 + 5 + a} {6}             &= 8 \\\\
\dfrac {40 + a} {6}                             &= 8 \\\\
40 + a                                          &= 8 \times 6 \\\\
40 + a                                          &= 48 \\\\
a                                               &= 48 - 40 \\\\
a                                               &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

Score of sixth pupil $= 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let the score of sixth pupil $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {9 + 10 + 9 + 7 + 5 + a} {6}             &= 8 \\\\
\dfrac {40 + a} {6}                             &= 8 \\\\
40 + a                                          &= 8 \times 6 \\\\
40 + a                                          &= 48 \\\\
a                                               &= 48 - 40 \\\\
a                                               &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Score of sixth pupil $= 8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question question'>

The number of goals scored by a Tonbridge hockey team in their four matches last season were: $4,\:\: 6,\:\: 1,\:\: 6$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
<div class='working placeholder'>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the mean number of goals scored.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean}     &= \dfrac {4 + 6 + 1 + 6} {4} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {17} {4} \\\\
                &= 4.25
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4.25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean}     &= \dfrac {4 + 6 + 1 + 6} {4} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {17} {4} \\\\
                &= 4.25
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4.25$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the median number of goals scored.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


Let's arrange the goal scored in ascending order.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Goal scored}           &= 4, \quad 6, \quad 1, \quad 6 \\\\
\text{Ordered goal scored}   &= 1, \quad \maroonC
{4}, \quad \maroonC
{6}, \quad 6 \\\\
\text{Median}                &= \dfrac {4 + 6} {2} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {10} {2} \\\\
                             &= 5
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_median
<emptyline>
Let's arrange the goal scored in ascending order.
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Goal scored}           &= 4, \quad 6, \quad 1, \quad 6 \\\\
\text{Ordered goal scored}   &= 1, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{4}, \quad \rtq_katex_color_info{6}, \quad 6 \\\\
\text{Median}                &= \dfrac {4 + 6} {2} \\\\
                             &= \dfrac {10} {2} \\\\
                             &= 5
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

What is the range of the number of goals scored?

</div>
<div class='workings'>

ABBR: $\text{Range} = \text{Highest number} - \text{Lowest number}$

                &= 5
\end {aligned}

</div>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean}     &= \dfrac {4 + 8 + 9} {3} \\\\

                &= \dfrac {7 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

$7$

<div class='question question'>

**Rainfall**

The table shows information about the rainfall in two places in South America.

| Place     | Season     |    Mean rainfall        | Number of months     | Months          |
|:-----:    |:------:    |:-------------------:    |:----------------:    |-------------    |
|   A       |   Dry      | $10\, cm$ per month     |        $8$           |  Jan to Aug     |
|           |   Wet      | $20\, cm$ per month     |        44$           | Sept to Dec     |
$\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:\:$

|       |         |                        |          |                 |
|---    |-----    |--------------------    |------    |-------------    |
| B     | Dry     | $5\,cm$ per month      | $10$     | July to Apr     |
|       | Wet     | $50\,cm$ per month     | $2$      | May to June     |


Which of the places has **more rainfall** on average over the whole year? 

Show working to explain your answer.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Tick $(\checkmark )$ your answer.

$\Box A\:\:\:\:\:\Box B$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mean of place A}      &= \dfrac {10 \times 8 + 20 \times 4} {8 + 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {80 + 80} {12} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {160} {12} \\\\
                            &= 13.3 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Mean of place B}      &= \dfrac {5 \times 10 + 50 \times 2} {8 + 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {50 + 100} {12} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {150} {12} \\\\
                            &= 12.5 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Place A has more rainfall on average compared to place B.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

A

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$

\text{Mean of place A}      &= \dfrac {10 \times 8 + 20 \times 4} {8 + 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {80 + 80} {12} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {160} {12} \\\\
                            &= 13.3 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{Mean of place B}      &= \dfrac {5 \times 10 + 50 \times 2} {8 + 4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {50 + 100} {12} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {150} {12} \\\\
                            &= 12.5 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Place A has more rainfall on average compared to place B.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
